ParlaMint-AT_2020-01-22-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00010_d7e5286	High house! Mr President. As follows, let me refer to the exciting statements by Klubobmann Kickl on the topic of totalitarian tendencies at universities: Universities are undoubtedly places of free scientific research and teaching. “Science and its teaching is free”, it is in the state law and in large letters at the rise of the New Institute building. Universities are also bound by the “multiplicity of scientific and artistic theories, methods and teaching opinions”, as is the 2002 target section of the UG. In addition, universities are places of intellectual confrontation and democratic discourse. The exchange of arguments, the weighing of the same and the possible finding of common positions, but possibly also the dissertation belong to the essence of a university. This dispute is always a spiritual one, never a violent one. This is extremely important to me, ladies and gentlemen! [[I should like to thank the rapporteur, Mr President, for his excellent report and for his excellent report.]] The freedom of science and freedom of expression clearly have their legal limits, which are determined by the rule of law and also by moral regulations: Prohibition law, slander, but in the field of science and research freedom, experiments on the human genome would also be included. If these laws and borders are respected, science and freedom of expression is fully given and must be guaranteed, especially at universities, because, as I have said, they are places of debate, of intellectual debate – and that is why these universities are so important to me. It is therefore no question, Mr Kickl, that an Alice Schwarzer may speak at the University of Applied Art, even though some of the activists to be attributed to the left camp and some of the ÖH representatives blame their anti-Muslim racism and disturb the event by minute-long counter-talk in scream strength, as Lisa Nimmerer fully reported. It must also be possible that a university course entitled “Flee, asylum, migration” can be held at the University of Klagenfurt, even though the activists more likely to be allocated to the right-wing camp were not in agreement with the asylum and immigration policy of 2016, the event was disrupted and the rector was hit by a fist. It must also be possible for Professor Höbelt to give a lecture on the subject The Second Republic in the lecture hall 50 of the University of Vienna, even if citizens are sitting in the audience and some ÖH officials are refusing to teach for political and world-wide reasons. In principle, I should also like to refer to the legal situation as regards the sharing of responsibility between the University and the Ministry. First: Habilitated – and Höbelt is a Habilitated person in this case – clearly have the task and also the right to represent their subject, which is defined by the Venia Legendi, independently in teaching and research. Habilitated people can offer courses whose curriculumal accountability – explained for the specialists – is then decided by the study programme managers. Courses of non-habilitated persons, in particular externals, must be approved and commissioned by the study programmes and the deanates. Secondly, If students consider that content that exceeds the limits of the legally permissible person would be represented in the course, they are entitled to communicate this to the rectorate or to provide ad themselves. The rectorate will usually always follow such accusations, but it is clear that they must be concrete statements and content. This does not suffice with lumpsums or statements by students that the professor or the professor does not suit them. Clearly, students have the right to objectly criticize teaching opinions and political positions. The critical and evidence-based discussion in the universities is a very important concern for us. However, you do not have the right to decide when a certain course may take place at a university or who may attend it. This is exclusively for the study programme lines and the deanates. Thirdly, In ensuring an orderly teaching establishment – and that is what you, Mr Kickl, have criticized here – and thus also in connection with the exercise of house rights, the rectors have the crucial function. The rectors at an autonomous university must ensure that house law is exercised and that courses properly announced by the Habilitated can also be carried out. The rectorate may also order security services, it may adopt a corresponding house order, and in case of need one can also issue house bans. The rectorate can obviously also attract the police if the rector believes that it requires the situation. Fourth: If the rectorate becomes regular in the performance of this task, a so-called replacement plan by the University Council shall be considered. The University Council is a kind of supervisory body within the university. Members have a very important role to play in this. You can ask the RAs: What happened to this class? They can obtain information, and they can also act if they feel that rektorates are not acting. Especially in the case of the University of Vienna, I can remind you: University councils are always broad. The last Turkish-blue government I have heard also has two representatives in the Council, who have been nominated by your group. High house! Universities are the places of debate, dispute, intellectual dispute and thus also part of a general democratic discourse. My ministry and I will do everything in our power to ensure what my idea is and what I have already indicated. It belongs to – if you want to – Raison d’être, for the reason of existence, a university, to maintain the free, factual and rational discourse and to deal with other opinions. We will do everything together with the rectors and the universities to ensure this. We take care and will take supervisory action when we see that the university bodies are acting unlawfully. We will also consult the ÖH on recent incidents regarding compliance with the provisions of the Austrian High School Law. [[Abg. Kickl: A sharp sword!]] I conclude with the famous Voltaire sentence, which I fully agree with: “My Lord, I do not share your opinion, but I would give my life to let you express it.” – Thank you.

I also expressed my support at Ecofin yesterday for the fact that, together with colleagues in Europe, we are insisting that there is an effective digital taxation at OECD level. There are just talks, and I hope that solutions will come quickly. If that is not the case, we want to move this together at European level. But we are not even satisfied with this, because we have already decided on a digital tax in the last government as one of the pioneers within the European Union. This has been in force since 1.1., you know, and we are one of the few countries in Europe that have made a first step towards greater tax justice by issuing a tax on advertising in the digital space and – very, very important! – have introduced a reporting obligation for online platforms as regards transactions, which would be the basis for possible taxation. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Double builder]] Thus, large multinational online companies have to pay as much taxes as domestic companies. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and MEPs of the Greens.]] Let me also briefly replicate two issues that you have raised. [[towards Abg. Rendi-Wagner]] – The reason why the difference between the unemployment rates of Greece and Germany is so great is not that the Greeks have taxed too little, but the reason was that they have made too many debts. This should also be a thought for all of us, namely that we should put our budget in order, because then what happened in Greece does not happen to us, namely that one is no longer master of his own budget, but then the Parliament can always decide what more money is spent on according to the budget. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Scherak.]] That is, debt discipline is announced, especially in terms of high unemployment rates, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] I may come to the subject of financial transaction tax which you have referred to. [[towards Abg. Rendi-Wagner]] Well, we have made a very clear point in the government programme, namely - I can quote it -: “The Federal Government is aimed at better “taxing” highly speculative financial products, especially derivatives and high-frequency trading activities. However, the current proposal of the FTT Group does not meet this claim, but does not affect domestic companies on the international capital market. Austria will “act” at EU level for the implementation of a targeted FTT – i.e. financial transaction tax. That is what I said yesterday in Brussels. In order to better understand the debate, let us perhaps take a look back: What was the origin for the idea of such a financial transaction tax? – The original idea was that we wanted to reduce the excess we had experienced in the run-up to the global financial and economic crisis. That is to say, responsible, highly speculative behaviour, foreign currency transactions that have been wagered for the decline of entire economies should be punished. To take the speculators to the line and also to prefer and support the real economy and investors in real companies, that was the idea. However, the current proposal has not remained much of this objective. The current proposal only provides more for the acquisition of shares to be taxed, and that is actually exactly the opposite of what you always wanted to achieve, because that means that it will be punished if Austrians participate in Austrian companies, but those speculators who make most of the derivative transactions, high-frequency trading, intraday trade are left outside. That is, the current proposal is exactly the opposite of what was originally intentioned. It was intentioned to punish speculators and prefer the real economy; this proposal makes the opposite: He punishes the real economy and almost rewards the speculators. That is why I have also said that if nothing changes in this proposal, Austria will leave this group because we are in favour of a broad taxation of financial transactions, a strengthening of the real economy and a punishment of speculation. This is what we have enshrined in the government programme, and we are in favour of it, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations on ÖVP and Greens.]] It is also counterproductive for another reason to want to implement the current proposal, because if we look at what the interest rate level is doing with the money of the local savers, then this is an alarming signal. Interests will probably remain low for the foreseeable future – and the most popular investment form for private money in Austria is still the savings book. 40 percent of private money is on savings books, but that means it will continuously be less worth it. I have just read an article in which Mr Zadrazil, the board of directors of a large Austrian bank, meant that this costs the savers in Austria 3 billion euros a year. While it was still possible in the 1990s that saving assets had doubled on average every 12 years at a rate of 6, 7 percent, today 400 years is needed to double the money, and this is a sluggish expropriation of the Austrians. That's where we have to counter it. We have therefore also established in the government programme that we want to make the capital market, the investment in shares, the saving of shares more attractive, by again discussing a retention period and thereby abolishing the capital income tax afterwards, so that it can be saved and provided for old age. This is an investment in real policy, as we understand in this government, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] I also have a certain contradiction in the SPÖ's position on this issue, because with some surprise I read yesterday the issue in which you criticise our attitude to the financial transaction tax – and that, although Mr Krainer said exactly the same thing in December, I just said, that the current proposal, as it is on the table, is unacceptable and that he is actually the opposite of what was originally intentioned. The fact that you have changed this attitude now and would somehow be in favour of taxing only the real economy and leaving the speculators outside, I must note that it is not just the attitude of this federal government. Thank you.

Point Declaration by the Federal Minister for Europe, Integration and Foreign Affairs pursuant to Article 19(2) of the Rules of Procedure of the National Council on ‘Cyber incidents in BMEIA’
ParlaMint-AT_2020-01-22-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00010_d7e10622	We're coming to the 2nd. Agenda item. Following this declaration, a debate will be held within the meaning of Article 81 of the Rules of Procedure, in accordance with the request submitted. I welcome the two Federal Ministers and give the floor to the Federal Minister for Europe, Integration and External Affairs. - Please, Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2020-01-22-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00010_d7e10649	Ladies and gentlemen! High house! It was my personal concern to take advantage of this opportunity to inform you today about the current state of the ongoing attack on the Ministry of Foreign Affairs' IT system. This is indeed a massive attack on our IT system. Unfortunately, such attacks, which can sometimes also move over long periods of time, are now no longer unusual. A number of other Member States have already faced similar attacks and have not yet received State aid. Directions and companies are not effective and regularly subject to such incidents. According to our knowledge today, this ongoing attack is a targeted cyber-attack against the Ministry of Foreign Affairs with the aim of obtaining information. It is important to clarify two essential points at the beginning: First: There has so far been no flow of information from the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. Secondly, At no time was the functioning of our worldwide consular services at risk. That is to say, the services of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs for citizens – be it at the embassies and consulates, whether it be on the homepage, via mail or in the various apps on the phones – were and are safe. Today, ladies and gentlemen, I can give you the following overview of what has been done: ==References== The attack on the internal IT system of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs on January has become known. Such attacks often run unnoticed for days, weeks or even months before you even get there. In this case, however, we were lucky that the IT team of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs recognized this attack within a few days and immediately launched countermeasures. Since the attack became known, a large team of several dozen experts has been working around the clock with high pressure to ward off this attack and restore full data security. But the Ministry of Foreign Affairs is not alone. From the beginning, the experts from my home worked closely with the experts of the Ministry of Interior, the state defense and the Federal Chancellery, with whom we also added external experts. This cross-ministerial team has been working around the clock for over two weeks now, you have to say, and tirelessly on solving the situation. I was also able to convince myself how well this cooperation between the Federal Offices works. Therefore, allow me to express at this point not only to the experts from my home, but above all to the employees of all the departments involved, especially the Ministry of Interior, the Ministry of Defense and the Federal Chancellery, my heartfelt thanks for the truly impressive work and the great commitment they show here. [[Congratulations on ÖVP and Greens.]] Especially in this case, it turns out again that security is a cross-sectional matter – security concerns all of us – and that a close cooperation in the defense of cyber-rat attacks is simply essential in our digitalized world. For this networked approach, the Network and Information System Security Act gives us the framework. An Interministerial Coordination Committee has been set up to discuss the operational measures to deal with the incident. A technical staff was formed, which is sent by different ministries. Since the data of the staff of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs may also be affected by this incident, they were informed of the incident in accordance with the provisions of the Data Protection Act so that they could take appropriate precautions themselves. The data protection authority was also used for security purposes. Of course, we have also used the European level. At the level of the European Union, the incident immediately became the EU Rapid Alert System. reported. It is, of course, self-evident that in this incident – and in the event of an incident of this size – we also work closely with our EU partners. I would therefore like to express my express thanks at this point for the great support we received from other Member States in this incident. [[Congratulations on ÖVP and Greens.]] Our experts are not only working to prevent and control the immediate attack, but of course they are also working to get clarity about the backgrounds and origin of the attack. At the moment, I have to say that quite openly, but there are not enough tangible points yet to be able to show clearly in one direction in order to speak absolutely dubiously and clearly of an authorship. In mid-February, this cyber-attack will be addressed here in the House of the Standing Subcommittee of the Committee on the Interior. There, Minister of the Interior Karl Nehammer and I will present the latest developments, share the latest information with you and discuss other aspects of this incident. However, I would like to ask you to understand that for technical and, above all, for the purposes of investigational tactics, I cannot announce any further details at the moment. Finally, let me clarify the following: The deletion work is very intense and highly professional, but we cannot report fire deleted yet. Overall, the state security networks have proved their worth. After the rapid discovery of the attack, the experts are now working with high pressure to reduce the consequences. Thirdly, and this is very important to me: The services, the services of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs for citizens, from the issue of emergency passports to the consulates and embassies to the travel warnings on our homepage, were guaranteed at any time. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and MEPs of the Greens.]] However, in the present case we are also going beyond immediate crisis management. We also use this incident to gain important lessons for the future security architecture of all federal offices. Today, the Ministry of Foreign Affairs is concerned, but it is important to draw the right conclusions in order to be able to protect all federal institutions even better in the future. The experience gained in the last days and weeks provides us with important insights. They allow improvements to be made for the IT security and the IT crisis management of the entire federal service in order to ensure that all services provided by the public at the IT level, which citizens rightly expect and demand from us, are always available and are secure in the future. – Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-01-22-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00010_d7e10844	Madam President. Mr. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to start by thanking the Federal Minister for his report here in front of the House today. I must also ensure that the services provided by the Ministry of Foreign Affairs are provided to all citizens of the Union and to all the security experts who have tried to control the damage in the last few days in the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. I would like to thank you, Mr President, for your excellent work. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and MEPs of the Greens.]] Cybersecurity does not concern us just recently or since the last year, in which some of the attacks concerning us have taken place. We often only see the tip of the iceberg. Attacks on the IT systems of companies, NGOs or public institutions are on the agenda. It was only recently that a hospital in the Czech Republic was paralysed by extortion software – a damage especially to patients. Similarly, a port in the U.S. has been shut down for 30 hours because of the fact that exactly such a malicious software has encrypted data, which is why the freight transfer could not take place. We remember 2015 when the German Bundestag was attacked. At that time, experts have tried for months to get hold of the situation, which in the end has led to some 20,000 PCs being disposed of, because until the end of the day, it could not be ensured that the malware has really disappeared from the devices. In Austria, an airport was faced with a variety of digital requests in 2016, namely the aim of overburdening the systems. This would have been devastating if it had actually been possible. Of course, last year's attacks are to be mentioned, above all, on political parties, namely on us, the ÖVP, but also on the SPÖ. Despite the fact that many things happen to ward off such attacks, one has to repeat every time that there is no 100% security in the IT field. Even as a small and neutral country, we are the target of attacks. It's a big challenge, it's mainly a cat-and-mouse game between attackers and defenders of the IT infrastructure; if more or less professional attackers include malicious software in the systems, then it spreads like cancer ulcers and despite treatment, there can be another attack if not all cancer cells are caught. This makes it particularly difficult to become a master of the problem. Which is perhaps even more terrifying: You don't have to be a professional in this field today in order to be able to attack. At $225 you get blackmail software to get 60 dollars per hour you get a DDOS attack on a server of your choice. You just need to show your finger. Cybercrime as a service, but also own hackers are used by criminal organizations, but also by states to pursue their purposes. It is particularly important to work together to address this rising danger situation. In the current case, the experts in the Ministry of Foreign Affairs are doing this with those from the Departments of Cybersecurity in the Interior and the State Defense, but also with external security companies, and especially with the European level, with our partners in the EU Member States. The basis for this – and the Minister mentioned this – is the Network and Information System Security Act, which was adopted in 2018, which is currently promoting more cooperation, including at EU level, and which allows for an improved data situation in order to control remedial action. We know, however, that there is still a lot to be done in Austria, which we are also committed to in this government programme for 2020 to 2024. We know there is a great lack of IT professionals in Austria. This exists in many IT areas, but also in the field of cybersecurity. That is why we want to strengthen training. We want above all to motivate young women to take a foothold in this area, and we want to create new offers, for example, a higher technical educational institution for cybersecurity. We focus on training in the public service, especially in executives, specializations at the police in terms of cybercrime towards cybercops and we want to expand and strengthen regional contacts in the individual districts. We want to get IT specialists through scholarship systems also in public service and link them to us in the long term. We must also act preventively, that is, to clarify the dangers and opportunities to protect ourselves. It is also central to develop research and development in this field, together with our partners at universities, but also in non-university institutions. We must be courageous when it comes to uniform safety standards, especially in interdisciplinary fields: 5G, artificial intelligence, internet of things and the like. In recent weeks there has been a great deal of discussion about who is behind the attack on the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. I have already tried to explain why it is so difficult to find out. In the first moment, of course, the cleaning of the systems is necessary, the services need to be maintained, and that also means time. You can also see from the example of Germany how long that took there. I also see, without the necessary evidence, today is not the time to speculate on the attackers or to ask whether the attack was politically motivated. However, I would like to make it clear that, whether we are here or the Federal Government, even if it was state actors, even if the attack is politically motivated, we cannot be deterred from it and we will not be scared either. We will continue to express our opinion, and we will continue to take our view, in our responsibility for Austria.

We're even going one step further: Not only do we want to create a uniform framework for personal assistance in all areas, but we also want to make it independent of the kind of disability, which is a very crucial point. Neither the place of residence nor the type of disability may decide whether someone gets personal assistance or not. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and MEPs from Greens and NEOS.]] The relevance of this motion for a resolution is therefore a little questionable, but we will, of course, agree with it, since we have also maintained it in the government programme. Just a short while to explain: In Austria, one still distinguishes between personal assistance and personal assistance at work. Personal assistance at the workplace is already regulated in a uniform manner, this is now also to be implemented in the personal assistance in all other areas. I am aware that this is a country matter, but the people out there who depend on assistance to get up in the morning to dress up to shape their day, it doesn't matter who is responsible as where. We simply have to succeed in establishing a regulation so that they too can lead a self-determined life, just as many people who have no disability do. It is also not only a ministry, because accessibility and inclusion are cross-sectional. In my opinion, an incentive system for businesses that employ people with disabilities would be more effective than impose sanctions. I think we are well advised to change that. I think it is good for the voluntary organizations – the Ministry – to work towards a change in this situation. For example, an idea would be that fire brigades or rescues are more devoted to women than to volunteers, while social services are increasingly seeking men as members and active ones. You know: because it’s 2020. On the other hand, there is also an educational gap. Formal volunteering is also linked to the degree of formal education. While 21 percent of people are voluntarily engaged in compulsory schooling, 45 percent are those with Matura or a higher education degree. Since it was a short issue in the Committee, it is also very important for me to point out that voluntary commitment is not a matter of origin. That also shows this report. What is evident is that the commitment of people with and without migration backgrounds is almost the same but different. People without a migration background are more active in formal organisations, people with migration backgrounds are more likely to be in informal structures. There is also a clear mission to us as a society, to us as a legal authority, also to the ministry to oppose it, so that in future, in the sense of integration, we can bring people with migration backgrounds more into the voluntary structures – free according to the motto: If you can see it, you can do it. There are corresponding demands from the Federal Youth Office, which I would like to point out. We should take this seriously. - Thank you.

Accordingly, today’s expenditure on households with children is no longer comparable to expenditure over 50 years ago. To finally arrive in 2020, we need a realistic child cost collection. With this proposal we can quickly take a first step for our families. We require a maintenance guarantee for children who receive no or very low maintenance or maintenance advance. With this measure we close a gap in the Austrian maintenance system, and we take a very, very important step towards combating child poverty in such a rich country as Austria.

Parliamentary democracy, however, is also an instrument that must constantly develop, because the need for people to engage politically changes, the intensity and opportunities change. It has developed an instrument which, in my opinion, is very, very interesting and well suited, namely that of popular desire. On the occasion of this request, which we have tabled within the framework of this initiative, we should all reflect together on how it is possible to combine the instrument of direct democracy with that of parliamentary, representative democracy so well that – perhaps I may use this term – the best of both democratic worlds can be combined. That is what this debate is all about. I have to tell you, I'm looking forward to it. There are already some ideas that are very interesting, for example popular desires with their own parliamentary seats. I would like to ask the Commission to take the view that it is a matter for the Council of Ministers to adopt a proposal for a directive on the approximation of the laws, regulations and administrative provisions of the Member States relating to the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to exposure to dangerous substances. Furthermore, there are ideas that can be interesting in which, I believe, one must pay very close attention to whether one introduces certain quotas, which then lead to certain obligations in the parliamentary procedure. We'll discuss all of this. I'm looking forward to it. I am pleased about good and many ideas and hope that we will succeed in making our democracy even more democratic in Austria. - Thank you.

The idea in the whole matter of digitization was and is that one can actually set up a parliamentary citizens' initiative virtually, i.e. collect these 500 signatures and also pass them on to the National Council virtually. It is no longer customary to use public authorities unless it is urgently necessary to go on the classical path, but it is now virtual. The National Council has to pick up. Our ideal condition is that of the citizen and the citizen, who is aware of her concern to the Members as well. I would like to thank the rapporteur, Mr President-in-Office of the Council, for his excellent report. This is simply not possible on a piece of paper in the appropriate form. We propose that – first of all with a limit of 2,000 signatures, that is, if one is to set up a citizens' initiative and actually manages to collect 2,000 signatures – one can also resolve the ticket with these 2,000 signatures and enter the debate with the Members directly in the Committee on Petitions. I told you earlier that history has begun in the era Faymann/Spindelegger 2013/2014. We then negotiated again during the last period, brought something back to the Committee on the Rules of Procedure, and it has just been postponed again. For more than six years now, I have been fighting for this – I was allowed to be the chairman of the Committee on Petitions – that we are really working intensively on how the National Council, regardless of ideology and party colours, can really open up more opportunities to get more married to parliamentarianism and citizen participation, to make more experienceable. That would be a huge added value for democracy. This would be evident to anyone who is interested in it. It is up to you here in the room to make this dream possible to 183 MEPs from many people outside. Thank you.
